Hi, doc. Well, I guess what finally got me in here is... over these last few months, my mind just keeps jumping all over the place.

Honestly, if I sit still for too long, I start feeling really antsy. I just... I'll spend my downtime rewriting my route plans, or I'll be calling to check on the new drivers after hours when I should be off the clock. And then when I actually get home, I'm constantly fixing things around the house. I'm not taking stupid risks on the road or making bad decisions or anything like that. I still know what I'm doing, I just have a lot of energy to burn.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting along with others at work or at home because of these symptoms?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely causing some friction. And a couple of my sales follow-ups just completely slipped through the cracks because I was chasing too many different ideas at once. \n\nThen at home... well, my wife and daughter have kind of been walking on eggshells around me lately. I'm trying to do all these projects around the house to help out, but I can tell I'm just adding more strain instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose so. For months now, I've been... well, either way too fired up, just feeling like everything is going to work out no matter what, or on the flip side, I'm just snapping over small stuff at home and out in the yard. It's really not my usual way of doing things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to finish tasks because your mind starts wandering to other things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's actually what finally got me to come in here. It's just really hard to stay on track and finish what I started.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these symptoms are affecting your relationships or work performance in a major way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was mentioning a few minutes ago, it really is. \n\nAt work, it's a pretty big problem when I have to ask dispatch or my clients to repeat details I should have caught the first time. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doc. Well, I guess what finally got me in here is... over these last few months, my mind just keeps jumping all over the place. \n\nI'll be out on the road and every little CB chirp, or a fuel price sign on the highway, or just some new idea popping into my head completely derails my focus. Because of it, I'm starting to miss details that I normally never miss. Things like a client’s delivery note, or forgetting who I promised to call back. It's just getting really frustrating and starting to mess with my work, so I figured I needed to talk to somebody about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless or fidgety, or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Honestly, if I sit still for too long, I start feeling really antsy. I just... I feel like I always need to be doing something. \n\nLately, I keep finding all these extra jobs for myself just to stay busy. I'll spend my downtime rewriting my route plans, or I'll be calling to check on the new drivers after hours when I should be off the clock. And then when I actually get home, I'm constantly fixing things around the house. It's just really hard for me to just sit back and relax right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulses or doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I know I'm keeping myself really busy right now, but I'm not... I'm not doing things without thinking them through. I'm not taking stupid risks on the road or making bad decisions or anything like that. I still know what I'm doing, I just have a lot of energy to burn.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting along with others at work or at home because of these symptoms?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely causing some friction. At work, I've had to ask dispatch and some of my clients to repeat details that I really should have tracked the first time around. And a couple of my sales follow-ups just completely slipped through the cracks because I was chasing too many different ideas at once. \n\nThen at home... well, my wife and daughter have kind of been walking on eggshells around me lately. I'm trying to do all these projects around the house to help out, but I can tell I'm just adding more strain instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose so. For months now, I've been... well, either way too fired up, just feeling like everything is going to work out no matter what, or on the flip side, I'm just snapping over small stuff at home and out in the yard. It's really not my usual way of doing things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to finish tasks because your mind starts wandering to other things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's actually what finally got me to come in here. Over these past few months, my mind just keeps jumping to every little thing. Every CB chirp, a fuel price sign on the highway, or just some new idea that pops into my head... it just pulls my attention right away. \n\nBecause of it, I'm missing details I normally never miss. I'll overlook a client's delivery note, or completely lose track of who I promised to call back. It's just really hard to stay on track and finish what I started.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these symptoms are affecting your relationships or work performance in a major way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was mentioning a few minutes ago, it really is. \n\nAt work, it's a pretty big problem when I have to ask dispatch or my clients to repeat details I should have caught the first time. And missing out on those sales follow-ups just because I was chasing too many different ideas at once... that definitely hurts my performance. \n\nAnd at home... well, like I said, my wife and daughter are basically walking on eggshells around me. I'm supposed to be helping out, but I can clearly tell I'm just adding a lot of strain to the house instead. So, yeah, I'd definitely say it's affecting both my job and my family in a major way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself talking more than usual or dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm a truck driver, so I spend a lot of time on my own in the cab anyway. When I am talking to people, like my wife or dispatch, I don't think I'm talking any more than I usually do. I haven't noticed myself taking over conversations or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's really the main reason I'm here. It's just... everything catches my attention lately. I'll be driving, and every little chirp on the CB radio or a random fuel price sign on the highway just pulls me right out of whatever I was doing or thinking about. \n\nAnd it's causing me to miss things I shouldn't. I'll get distracted and completely overlook a delivery note from a client, or I'll just forget who I was supposed to call back. It's like my mind just jumps to the next thing before I can finish the first.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these distractions and restlessness throughout the entire day, or are there specific times when they seem worse?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a specific time, to be honest. It just... it kind of feels like it happens all through the day. I haven't caught onto any pattern where it's worse in the morning or at night or anything like that. It's just sort of a constant thing lately, no matter what time it is.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these symptoms of distraction and restlessness?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about seven months now. That's right around the time my daughter moved back home. I've just been... trying to keep everything steady since then, and that's when I really started noticing all this.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or distressed by these symptoms of distraction and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say I am. It's definitely causing some problems. At work, I've had to ask dispatch and my clients to repeat details that I really should have been able to keep track of. And... a couple of my sales follow-ups just slipped through the cracks because my brain was just chasing too many ideas at once. \n\nIt's not just work, either. At home, my wife and daughter... they've been kind of walking on eggshells around me. I can tell I'm just adding strain to the house right now, when I'm really supposed to be the one helping since my daughter moved back. It's just frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these symptoms are interfering with your ability to enjoy your daily activities or maintain a healthy relationship with your family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I would. Like I was just saying, the family part is... well, it's definitely strained. It's hard to have a normal, healthy relationship when they feel like they have to tiptoe around me all the time. \n\nAnd as for enjoying my daily activities... I guess I haven't really thought about it exactly like that, but yeah. It's pretty tough to just sit back and enjoy my day when I'm this wound up. I'm mostly just focused on trying to get through my driving routes and not make any more mistakes, so there isn't really a lot of room left to just enjoy things right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or overly self-critical about the mistakes you've been making or the strain you're causing at home and work?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I wouldn't really say that. \n\nI mean, it bothers me, sure. I don't like messing up with dispatch or making things tense for my wife. But I'm not... I'm not sitting around beating myself up over it or anything. It's more just frustrating because I know I can do my job better than this, and I just want to get things back on track at the house. So, no, not really guilty or down on myself. Just annoyed by the whole situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping or noticing any changes in your sleep patterns over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has definitely changed lately. Most nights, I'm only getting maybe three or four hours of sleep. The weird thing is, I'll wake up way before daylight, and instead of feeling worn out from not sleeping enough, I just feel completely ready to roll. It's like my body doesn't even need the rest right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms are severe enough that they're impacting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go quite that far. I mean... my overall quality of life is still okay. \n\nLike I said, it's definitely frustrating right now with the tension at home and having to double-check everything on my routes, but... I'm still working, you know? I'm still getting through the day and doing what I need to do. So, no, I wouldn't say it's severe. It's just... a bit of a bumpy patch right now because I'm so wired all the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current situation as significantly impairing your daily functioning?\n\nPatient: Well, it's definitely causing some issues. At work, I've had to ask dispatch and some of my clients to repeat details that I really should have been able to keep track of. And, uh, a couple of my sales follow-ups just slipped through the cracks because my head was going in too many directions at once with all these different ideas. \n\nAnd then at home... it's tense. My wife and daughter, they've been kind of walking on eggshells around me. I can tell I'm just adding strain to the house when I'm supposed to be the one helping out. So, yeah, it's getting in the way of things, for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms are causing significant problems in your daily life that you cannot manage on your own?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say it's at a point where I can't manage it. I mean... like I said before, I'm still getting my routes done, still paying the bills. \n\nIt's definitely a hassle right now, and I know I'm probably getting on my family's nerves with how wired I am, but... I don't feel like I've completely lost control of things or need someone to step in and take over. I'm still handling my business. I just need to figure out how to dial it back a notch, you know?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I haven't noticed myself taking over conversations or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's really the main reason I'm here. It's just... everything catches my attention lately. I'll be driving, and every little chirp on the CB radio or a random fuel price sign on the highway just pulls me right out of whatever I was doing or thinking about. \n\nAnd it's causing me to miss things I shouldn't. I'll get distracted and completely overlook a delivery note from a client, or I'll just forget who I was supposed to call back. It's like my mind just jumps to the next thing before I can finish the first.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these distractions and restlessness throughout the entire day, or are there specific times when they seem worse?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a specific time, to be honest. It just... it kind of feels like it happens all through the day. I haven't caught onto any pattern where it's worse in the morning or at night or anything like that. It's just sort of a constant thing lately, no matter what time it is.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these symptoms of distraction and restlessness?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about seven months now. That's right around the time my daughter moved back home. I've just been... trying to keep everything steady since then, and that's when I really started noticing all this.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or distressed by these symptoms of distraction and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say I am. At home, my wife and daughter... they've been kind of walking on eggshells around me. I can tell I'm just adding strain to the house right now, when I'm really supposed to be the one helping since my daughter moved back. It's just frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these symptoms are interfering with your ability to enjoy your daily activities or maintain a healthy relationship with your family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I would. I guess I haven't really thought about it exactly like that, but yeah. It's pretty tough to just sit back and enjoy my day when I'm this wound up. I'm mostly just focused on trying to get through my driving routes and not make any more mistakes, so there isn't really a lot of room left to just enjoy things right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or overly self-critical about the mistakes you've been making or the strain you're causing at home and work?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I wouldn't really say that. \n\nI mean, it bothers me, sure. I don't like messing up with dispatch or making things tense for my wife. But I'm not... So, no, not really guilty or down on myself. Just annoyed by the whole situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping or noticing any changes in your sleep patterns over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has definitely changed lately. Most nights, I'm only getting maybe three or four hours of sleep. The weird thing is, I'll wake up way before daylight, and instead of feeling worn out from not sleeping enough, I just feel completely ready to roll. It's like my body doesn't even need the rest right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your symptoms are severe enough that they're impacting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go quite that far. I mean... my overall quality of life is still okay. \n\nLike I said, it's definitely frustrating right now with the tension at home and having to double-check everything on my routes, but... It's just... a bit of a bumpy patch right now because I'm so wired all the time.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current situation as significantly impairing your daily functioning?\n\nPatient: Well, it's definitely causing some issues. I just need to figure out how to dial it back a notch, you know?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your symptoms are currently causing significant distress or impairment in social, occupational, or other important areas of functioning?\n\nPatient: Well, like I've been saying, it's definitely causing some friction at home, and work's been a bit of a challenge lately with my focus being all over the place.
